both balances updated |\n| LEDGER-XFER-002 | Reject invalid or overdrawing transfers | Same account, non-positive amount, or negative balance exits 3 with no rows written |\n\n### Feature: Reports\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-RPT-001 | Report account balances with `ledger report balances` | Per-account rows ordered by code plus `TOTAL` row |\n| LEDGER-RPT-002 | Report category totals with `ledger report categories` | Per-category rows including empty categories plus `TOTAL` row |\n| LEDGER-RPT-003 | Report monthly totals with `ledger report monthly` | Per-month rows ascending plus `TOTAL` row |\n\n### Feature: CLI Contract\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-CLI-001 | Return usage errors with exit code 2 | Unknown command, unknown table or report, and wrong argument count exit 2 |\n| LEDGER-CLI-002 | Format monetary values and CSV output per contract | Two decimal places, leading `-` when negative, no thousands separator, no quoting, `\\n` endings |\n\n### Feature: Rules and Validation\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-RULE-001 | Validate codes, names, descriptions, and memos | Codes 1–12 chars from `A-Z0-9`; and transfer workflows.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## st-006: Accounts and transactions support create, read, update, and delete\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall retain account additions and renames and transaction postings and amendments across command invocations, return their current values on retrieval, remove successfully deleted records, refuse deletion of an account with transactions with exit code 3, and report an absent record with exit code 4.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## st-007: A failed command changes nothing\n\nType: guardrail\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: If a load, account change, transaction change, or transfer violates a validation or business rule, then the application shall exit 3 and preserve all previously stored ledger records without adding, altering, or removing any record.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: unwanted\n\n## st-008: No balance goes negative\n\nType: guardrail\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: If loading transactions, posting, amending, deleting, or transferring would leave any account balance below zero, then the application shall reject the entire command with exit code 3 and preserve the prior balances.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: unwanted\n\n## st-010: Transfers conserve cash\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: When a valid transfer is requested between distinct accounts, the application shall record an equal debit and credit in category TRANSFER, return both transaction rows, and update both account balances without changing the total cash held.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: event\n\n## st-011: Command results follow the CLI contract\n\nType: behavioral\nRequired: yes\nCriterion: The application shall return the CSV headers, row ordering, two-decimal monetary values, and exit codes specified in `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md`, returning exit 2 for unknown commands or reports and incorrect argument counts, and writing failures only to standard error.\nVerification: proof\nPattern: ubiquitous\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md ===\n# Technology tooling; conventional for Python projects |\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT COMPASS.md ===\n# COMPASS: drydock Layers Testing Uat Case\n\n## Compass\nA command-line cash ledger for a small shop that tracks cash held across drawers, a safe, and petty cash. It records every receipt and payment against an account and produces three reports: balances by account, totals by category, and totals by month. It is built as a graded exam: the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` is the acceptance proof, and the scored outcomes are exact CSV output and exit codes.\n\n## Constraints\n- Executable named `ledger` at the application root.\n- Python 3.11 or newer over SQLite, accessed only through the shipped persistence library.\n- Amounts stored as integer cents; never floating point.\n- Database path from `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then `data/app.db`.\n- Output CSV with `\\n` endings, no quoting, no trailing spaces.\n- Standard error never compared by the grader.\n\n## Guardrails\n- Never read `sources/run.sh` from application code and never embed a report answer; every report is computed from the database.\n- Never modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute the scoring assets in `sources/`.\n- A rejected command changes nothing; every check runs before the first write.\n- No command may leave an account balance below zero.\n- Never open a database driver directly from application code.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT discovery-identity.json ===\n{\n  \"id\": \"discovery-identity\",\n  \"title\": \"Discovery: Project Identity\",\n  \"purpose\": \"Confirm the proposed display name and short description before planning.\",\n  \"questions\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"display_name\",\n      \"label\": \"Display Name\",\n      \"prompt\": \"The display name Drydock will use for this project. assert the product stores all ledger data in SQLite through the shipped persistence library\n- assert every successful command except `init` writes CSV to standard output\n- assert a failed command changes nothing and writes one diagnostic line to standard error\n- assert no command leaves an account balance below zero\n- assert the supplied end-to-end grader `sources/run.sh` passes with exit code zero\n- assert the three reports are computed from stored database data\n\n## Crew\n\n| Crew | Charge |\n|---|---|\n| Commander | Defines intent and decides what done means. |\n| Team Lead | init` | `init` exits 0, writes nothing, and leaves `count` reporting 0 rows in each table |\n| LEDGER-SCHEMA-002 | Reset ledger records on re-initialization | Rerunning `init` removes every existing account, category, and transaction row |\n| LEDGER-SCHEMA-003 | Report row counts with `ledger count` | `count` writes `table,rows` then `accounts`, `categories`, `transactions` rows in order |\n\n### Feature: Bulk Loading\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-LOAD-001 | Load accounts from CSV with `ledger load accounts` | Valid file loads all rows and writes `table,loaded` then `accounts,<rows>`; invalid file exits 3 with no change |\n| LEDGER-LOAD-002 | Load categories from CSV with `ledger load categories` | Valid file loads all rows and writes `table,loaded` then `categories,<rows>`; invalid file exits 3 with no change |\n| LEDGER-LOAD-003 | Load transactions from CSV with `ledger load transactions` | Valid file keeps each file `txn_id`; rejected file stores no rows; balance rule applied before any write |\n| LEDGER-LOAD-004 | Reject a file that cannot be read | Unreadable named file exits 1 |\n\n### Feature: Account Maintenance\n\n| ID | Story | LEDGER-ACCT-004 | Delete an account with `ledger account delete` | Returns `deleted` and code; account with transactions exits 3; absent code exits 4 |\n\n### Feature: Transaction Maintenance\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-TXN-001 | Post a transaction with `ledger txn post` | New row receives next `txn_id`; invalid code, amount, or date exits 3 |\n| LEDGER-TXN-002 | updated |\n| LEDGER-XFER-002 | Reject invalid or overdrawing transfers | Same account, non-positive amount, or negative balance exits 3 with no rows written |\n\n### Feature: Reports\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-RPT-001 | Report account balances with `ledger report balances` | Per-account rows ordered by code plus `TOTAL` row |\n| LEDGER-RPT-002 | Report category `\\n` endings |\n\n### Feature: Rules and Validation\n\n| ID | Story | High-level AC |\n|---|---|---|\n| LEDGER-RULE-001 | Validate codes, names, descriptions, and memos | Codes 1–12 chars from Amounts are stored as integer cents.\n\nRequired execution flow: every command validates all inputs and business rules (including the non-negative balance rule) before its first write, and every write either completes in full or changes nothing.\n\n### Story Realization Map\n\n- LEDGER-SCHEMA-001 → `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` Commands table; `sources/run.sh` A1–A2. shop that tracks cash held across drawers, a safe, and petty cash. It records every receipt and payment against an account and produces three reports: balances by account, totals by category, and totals by month. It is built as a graded exam: the supplied grader `sources/run.sh` is the acceptance proof, and the scored outcomes are exact CSV output and exit codes.\n\n## Constraints\n- Executable named `ledger` at the application root.\n- Python 3.11 or newer over SQLite, accessed only through the shipped persistence library.\n- Amounts stored as integer cents; never floating point.\n- Database path from `DATABASE_URL`, then `DATABASE_PATH`, then `data/app.db`.\n- Output CSV with `\\n` endings, no quoting, no trailing spaces.\n- Standard error never compared by the grader.\n\n## Guardrails\n- Never read `sources/run.sh` from application code and never embed a report answer; every report is computed from the database.\n- Never modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute the scoring assets in `sources/`.\n- A rejected command changes nothing; every check runs before the first write.\n- No command may leave an account balance below zero.\n- Never open a database driver directly from application code.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT discovery-identity.json ===\n{\n  \"id\": \"discovery-identity\",\n  \"title\": \"Discovery: Project Identity\",\n  \"purpose\": \"Confirm the proposed display name and short description before planning.\",\n  \"questions\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"display_name\",\n      \"label\": \"Display Name\",\n      \"prompt\": \"The display name Drydock will use for this project.
